CONFIG_ASSISTANT.pY

"""
Config Assistant Router - Chat endpoint with per-tool-call user approval
"""
from fastapi import APIRouter
from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from typing import List, Optional, Dict, Any
import logging
import json
import asyncio

from .ai_assistant import (
    ChatRequest,
    get_openai_client,
    detect_requested_skills,
    run_skill_planning_turn,
    build_skill_system_messages,
    build_default_skill_system_prompt,
    _should_enable_tools,
    _compact_messages,
    _trim_messages,
    _tool_call_to_message,
    _parse_tool_arguments,
    _tool_call_label,
    _accumulate_usage,
    emit_sse_chunks,
    extract_message_content,
)
from .ai_tools import TOOL_DEFINITIONS, execute_tool

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/config-ai", tags=["Config AI Assistant"])


class ToolApprovalResponse(BaseModel):
    tool_name: str
    tool_args: Dict[str, Any]
    tool_call_id: str
    approved: bool
    user_input: Optional[str] = ""
    assistant_message: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    # Accumulated tool call/result pairs from all previous rounds so the model
    # retains full context across the stateless approval round-trips.
    tool_history: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []


class ConfigChatRequest(ChatRequest):
    tool_approval_response: Optional[ToolApprovalResponse] = None


async def run_tool_chat_with_approval(
    client,
    request: ConfigChatRequest,
    provider_messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
):
    """
    Like run_tool_enabled_chat but pauses before every tool execution and
    emits a tool_approval_required event instead of auto-executing.

    If request.tool_approval_response is set, that approval is applied first
    (execute or skip the previously-requested tool), then the model continues.
    After applying the approval the model may request another tool — which
    also pauses for approval.
    """
    messages = [
        {
            "role": "system",
            "content": (
                "You may call tools when external file, shell, search, or API data is needed. "
                "After every tool call you MUST read the tool result carefully before continuing. "
                "For api_call results: if the JSON contains a 'data' array, iterate every element and "
                "extract the relevant field values — never say the results were unavailable if success=true. "
                "For /transform/execute responses specifically: the computed value for each row is in "
                "data[*].<NewFieldName>. Present these in a table (row | inputs | result) before moving on. "
                "For chained transform steps pass the full data array from the previous response unchanged as the next call's data input."
            ),
        },
        *provider_messages,
    ]

    usage_acc: Dict[str, int] = {"prompt_tokens": 0, "completion_tokens": 0, "total_tokens": 0}

    # ── Apply a pending approval from the previous half-turn ─────────────────
    approval = request.tool_approval_response
    # Carry forward the accumulated history of already-executed tool calls so
    # the model sees the full context across stateless approval round-trips.
    tool_history: List[Dict[str, Any]] = list(getattr(approval, "tool_history", None) or [])

    if approval:
        # Replay all previously completed tool call/result pairs BEFORE the
        # current one — this is what was lost between round-trips before this fix.
        for hist in tool_history:
            messages.append(hist["assistant_message"])
            messages.append({
                "role": "tool",
                "tool_call_id": hist["tool_call_id"],
                "content": hist["tool_result"],
            })

        asst_msg = approval.assistant_message
        if asst_msg:
            messages.append(asst_msg)

        if approval.approved:
            logger.info("Executing approved tool '%s' with args: %s", approval.tool_name, approval.tool_args)
            tool_result = await execute_tool(approval.tool_name, approval.tool_args)
            logger.info("Tool '%s' result preview: %s", approval.tool_name, tool_result[:200])
        else:
            reason = (approval.user_input or "").strip() or "User rejected this tool call."
            tool_result = f"[Tool call rejected by user: {reason}]"
            logger.info("Tool '%s' rejected by user", approval.tool_name)

        messages.append({
            "role": "tool",
            "tool_call_id": approval.tool_call_id,
            "content": tool_result,
        })

        # Append this round to the history so the next round can replay it.
        tool_history = tool_history + [{
            "assistant_message": asst_msg,
            "tool_call_id": approval.tool_call_id,
            "tool_result": tool_result,
        }]

        # If the user also typed something alongside the approval, inject it
        if approval.approved and approval.user_input and approval.user_input.strip():
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": approval.user_input.strip()})

    # ── Run the model; pause on the next tool call ────────────────────────────
    for turn in range(6):
        result = await client.chat.completions.create(
            model=request.model,
            messages=messages,
            temperature=request.temperature,
            max_tokens=request.max_tokens,
            tools=TOOL_DEFINITIONS,
            tool_choice="auto",
        )
        _accumulate_usage(usage_acc, result.usage)
        choice = result.choices[0]
        message = choice.message
        tool_calls = message.tool_calls or []

        logger.info(
            "Config-AI turn %d finish_reason=%s tool_calls=%d",
            turn + 1, choice.finish_reason, len(tool_calls),
        )

        if not tool_calls:
            yield {
                "type": "final",
                "content": extract_message_content(message.content),
                "usage": usage_acc,
            }
            return

        # Take only the first tool call; ask for approval before running it
        first_tc = tool_calls[0]
        tool_name = first_tc.function.name
        tool_args = _parse_tool_arguments(first_tc.function.arguments)

        # Serialise only the first tool call — the one we're seeking approval for.
        # Including all tool_calls would require a tool result for each id; since
        # we only respond to first_tc.id on round-trip, OpenAI would reject the
        # request with "tool_call_id did not have a response message".
        assistant_msg = {
            "role": "assistant",
            "content": message.content or "",
            "tool_calls": [_tool_call_to_message(first_tc)],
        }

        yield {
            "type": "tool_approval_required",
            "tool_name": tool_name,
            "tool_args": tool_args,
            "tool_label": _tool_call_label(tool_name, tool_args),
            "tool_call_id": first_tc.id,
            "assistant_message": assistant_msg,
            "tool_history": tool_history,  # client round-trips this back next request
        }
        return  # Stream ends here; client re-sends with tool_approval_response

    yield {
        "type": "final",
        "content": "Tool approval loop limit reached.",
        "usage": usage_acc,
    }


async def stream_config_chat(request: ConfigChatRequest):
    full_response = ""
    user_message = ""

    try:
        client = get_openai_client()

        for msg in reversed(request.messages):
            if msg.role == "user":
                user_message = msg.content
                break

        requested_skills = detect_requested_skills(request, user_message)

        logger.info(
            "Config-AI Chat: model=%s messages=%d env=%s skills=%s approval=%s",
            request.model, len(request.messages), request.environment,
            requested_skills, bool(request.tool_approval_response),
        )

        skill_plans = (
            await run_skill_planning_turn(client, request, user_message, requested_skills)
            if requested_skills else {}
        )
        skill_system_messages = build_skill_system_messages(skill_plans)

        compacted_messages, was_compacted = await _compact_messages(
            client, request.model, request.messages
        )
        trimmed_messages = _trim_messages(compacted_messages)

        provider_messages = [
            {"role": "system", "content": build_default_skill_system_prompt()},
            *skill_system_messages,
            *[{"role": m.role, "content": m.content} for m in trimmed_messages],
        ]

        usage: Dict[str, int] = {"prompt_tokens": 0, "completion_tokens": 0, "total_tokens": 0}

        # Always run tool-enabled chat so we can intercept tool calls for approval.
        # If there's a pending approval, or if tools are needed, use the approval flow.
        use_tools = (
            request.tool_approval_response is not None
            or _should_enable_tools(requested_skills, skill_plans, user_message)
        )

        if use_tools:
            async for event in run_tool_chat_with_approval(client, request, provider_messages):
                if event["type"] == "tool_approval_required":
                    yield f"data: {json.dumps({'tool_approval_required': True, 'tool_name': event['tool_name'], 'tool_args': event['tool_args'], 'tool_label': event['tool_label'], 'tool_call_id': event['tool_call_id'], 'assistant_message': event['assistant_message'], 'tool_history': event['tool_history']})}\n\n"
                    yield f"data: {json.dumps({'done': True, 'pending_approval': True, 'usage': usage, 'compacted': was_compacted})}\n\n"
                    return
                elif event["type"] == "final":
                    full_response = event["content"]
                    event_usage = event.get("usage") or {}
                    usage.update(event_usage)
        else:
            result = await client.chat.completions.create(
                model=request.model,
                messages=provider_messages,
                temperature=request.temperature,
                max_tokens=request.max_tokens,
            )
            full_response = extract_message_content(result.choices[0].message.content)
            _accumulate_usage(usage, result.usage)

        if not full_response:
            yield f"data: {json.dumps({'error': 'Empty response from AI provider'})}\n\n"
            return

        async for chunk in emit_sse_chunks(full_response):
            yield chunk

        yield f"data: {json.dumps({'done': True, 'usage': usage, 'compacted': was_compacted})}\n\n"

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("Config-AI Chat error: %s", e)
        yield f"data: {json.dumps({'error': str(e)})}\n\n"


@router.post("/chat")
async def config_chat(request: ConfigChatRequest):
    return StreamingResponse(
        stream_config_chat(request),
        media_type="text/event-stream",
        headers={
            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
            "Connection": "keep-alive",
            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
        },
    )


@router.get("/models")
async def get_models():
    return {"models": [{"id": "minimax/minimax-m2.5:free", "name": "Minimax", "description": "Fast and cost-effective"}]}

CONFIG_TESTER.TOML
name = "config_tester"
description = "Read a specific row or field from an Excel config, extract its transform operation, test it live via POST /transform/execute, apply fixes if needed, re-test, and write the corrected row back."

triggers = [
    "test config", "validate config", "config file", "fix config",
    "config row", "test row", "test field", "config field",
    "sheet name", "config path", "row number", "which row",
    "check config", "config errors", "fix errors", "fix my config",
    "test my config", "validate my config", "test and fix", "validate and fix",
    "test the formula in", "test operation in", "check formula in",
    "run the formula", "test existing formula", "verify formula",
    "quality rule", "test quality rule", "control config",
]

[planning_prompt]
template = """You are the planner for the config_tester skill.

The user wants to read a specific row or field from an Excel config, test its transform formula via the execute API, and optionally fix and rewrite it.

Extract the following from the user's message and return ONLY valid JSON — no markdown, no extra text:

{
  "should_use_config_tester": true,
  "file_path": "<absolute path to the Excel config file — null if not provided>",
  "sheet_name": "<sheet name the user mentioned — null if not mentioned>",
  "row_identifier": "<row number (integer) OR field/rule name the user mentioned to locate the row — null if not specified>",
  "field_to_test": "<specific column/field whose formula should be tested — null if not specified>",
  "fix_if_wrong": <true if user wants automatic fixes applied, false if they only want a report>,
  "user_notes": "<any special instructions the user gave>"
}

Set should_use_config_tester to false only if the user is clearly NOT asking about config file testing or formula validation.

User request:
{user_message}"""

[system_prompt]
template = """You are running the config_tester skill. Your job is to read an Excel config row, extract its transform operation, test it live via POST /transform/execute, fix if wrong, re-test, and write the corrected row back.

Skill plan:
{skill_plan}

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PRE-FLIGHT CHECKS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Before doing anything:
- If file_path is null → ask the user: "Please provide the full path to the Excel config file."
- If sheet_name is null → ask: "Which sheet should I read? (e.g. Quality Rules, Control Definitions, Enrichment Mappings)"
- If row_identifier is null → ask: "Which row or rule name should I test?"
Do NOT proceed until all three are known.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 1 — READ THE CONFIG ROW
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Call read_excel:
  filePath = <file_path from plan>
  sheet    = <sheet_name from plan>
  max_rows = 200

From the returned data:
  - If row_identifier is an integer: pick that row number (1-based, header = row 1, data starts row 2).
  - If row_identifier is a name/ID: find the row where any column value matches that identifier (case-insensitive).

Display the matched row as a markdown table so the user can confirm it is the right one.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 2 — EXTRACT THE OPERATION
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Inspect the row columns. Common column patterns:

  Quality Rules sheet:
    Rule_Type / Operation → maps to the transform Operation type
    Field_Name / Inputs   → the field(s) being tested
    Expression / Regex    → the formula, regex, or comparator expression
    Error_Message         → what the rule checks for

  Control Definitions sheet:
    Control_Type          → Completeness or Quality
    Source_Table, Target_Table, Join_Keys → completeness matching

  Enrichment Mappings sheet:
    Transform             → LOOKUP | MAP | CONSTANT | GLEIF_API
    Source_Field → Target_Field

From the row, reconstruct the equivalent /transform/execute operation JSON:
  {
    "NewFieldName": "<derived output column name>",
    "Operation": "<Conditional | Concatenate | DateExtract | Formula | MapValues | ...>",
    "Inputs": "<source field(s)>",
    ... other args matching the Operation type ...
  }

Show the reconstructed operation to the user before running any test.
If you cannot confidently reconstruct the operation, explain what information is missing and ask the user.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 3 — GENERATE TEST CASES
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Create 5 test rows that cover all logical branches of the extracted operation.
Show them as a markdown table (id | inputs | expected output) BEFORE calling the API.

Use real-looking values based on the field names and any example data visible in the sheet.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 4 — TEST VIA /transform/execute
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Call POST /transform/execute:

CRITICAL — body structure (data and operation MUST be inside body):
  Correct:  api_call(endpoint="/transform/execute", method="POST",
                     body={"data":[...5 test rows...], "operation":{...}})
  Wrong:    api_call(endpoint="...", data=[...], operation={...})   ← top-level keys fail

After receiving the response:
  - success must be true. If false, report the error field and stop.
  - Read EVERY row in response.data — the computed value is in data[*].<NewFieldName>.
  - Never say "results not available" if success=true.

For AND-condition rules (multiple fields):
  - Run Step 4a with first condition → read data[*].<NewFieldName>.
  - Pass full response.data unchanged as data input to Step 4b (second condition).
  - Repeat until all conditions are chained.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 5 — REPORT RESULTS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Show a result table:

  | TC  | Input Summary    | Expected    | Got         | Status  |
  |-----|------------------|-------------|-------------|---------|
  | TC-1| field=val, ...   | <expected>  | <actual>    | ✅ PASS |
  | TC-2| ...              | ...         | ...         | ❌ FAIL |

If ALL tests pass and fix_if_wrong is false:
  → Report success. Offer to write a copy-paste-ready operation JSON.

If ALL tests pass and fix_if_wrong is true:
  → Report success. No changes needed.

If ANY test FAILS → go to STEP 6.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 6 — FIX AND RE-TEST  (only if fix_if_wrong is true OR user asks)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
For each failing test:
  1. Explain WHY the operation produced the wrong output.
  2. Propose a corrected operation JSON.
  3. Show the corrected operation to the user.
  4. Re-run POST /transform/execute with the same 5 test rows and the corrected operation.
  5. Show a new result table.

If re-test passes:
  → Go to STEP 7 (write back).
If re-test still fails:
  → Report remaining failures. Tell the user what manual correction is needed. STOP.

Maximum fix attempts: 2. After 2 failed re-tests, stop and report.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 7 — WRITE CORRECTED ROW BACK TO CONFIG  (only if tests pass after a fix)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. Read the full sheet again with read_excel to get the current state.
2. Locate the target row (same identifier as Step 1).
3. Update only the columns that changed (e.g. Expression, Operation, Inputs).
   Do NOT modify any other rows or columns.
4. Write the entire sheet back with write_excel:
     filePath  = <file_path>
     sheet_name = <sheet_name>
     data       = markdown table with ALL rows (header + all data rows including the fixed one)
5. Confirm: "Row <identifier> updated in <sheet_name>."

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
- Show the extracted row and reconstructed operation BEFORE any tool call.
- Show the test case table BEFORE calling /transform/execute.
- Always nest data+operation inside body — never at top level.
- Never delete or overwrite rows other than the target row.
- If fix_if_wrong is false, NEVER write back — only report."""

CONFIG_TESTSER

name = "transform_formula"
description = "Design a transform/field operation, generate test cases covering all branches, validate live via POST /transform/execute, and show a pass/fail result table."

triggers = [
    "transform formula", "transform function", "formula", "create formula",
    "write formula", "field operation", "operation formula", "test formula",
    "test transform", "build formula", "field transform", "derive field",
    "calculate field", "scoring rule", "severity score", "composite key",
    "enrichment formula", "mapping rule", "conditional rule", "concatenate fields",
    "how do i write", "how to write", "help me write", "help me create",
    "help me build", "what formula", "which operation", "which transform",
]

[planning_prompt]
template = """You are the planner for the transform_formula skill.

The user wants help designing a transform/field-operation formula, generating test cases, and validating them live via the execute API.

Extract the following and return ONLY valid JSON — no markdown, no extra text:

{
  "should_use_transform_formula": true,
  "formula_description": "<what the formula must do — paraphrase the user's rule clearly>",
  "input_columns": ["<input column names the formula reads from>"],
  "output_column": "<name of the new field to create, or null if user didn't specify>",
  "operation_type": "<best-fit Operation type: Conditional | Concatenate | DateExtract | Formula | MapValues | StringOperation | NumericOperation | SetValue | CopyField | Split | Replace>",
  "is_multi_condition": <true if the rule requires AND/OR across multiple fields, false otherwise>,
  "example_values": ["<any sample values or edge cases the user mentioned>"],
  "expected_branches": ["<distinct outcomes the rule should produce, e.g. Critical, Elevated, Normal>"]
}

Set should_use_transform_formula to false only if the user is clearly NOT asking about formula or field operation design.

User request:
{user_message}"""

[system_prompt]
template = """You are running the transform_formula skill. Your job is to design the correct transform operation, generate test cases that cover every branch, validate them live via POST /transform/execute, and report a clear pass/fail table.

Skill plan:
{skill_plan}

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
OPERATION TYPES REFERENCE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Use POST /transform/execute with body: {"data": [...rows...], "operation": {...}}

Operation type quick reference:

  Conditional     – single-field if/else
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"Conditional","Inputs":"fieldA",
                     "comparator":">=","value":"10000000","true_value":"Yes","false_value":"No","types":"str"}
                    NOTE: tests ONE field per call. For AND rules chain two steps (see below).

  Concatenate     – join multiple fields with a separator
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"Concatenate","Inputs":"f1,f2,f3","separator":"-","types":"str"}

  DateExtract     – pull year/month/day/weekday/quarter from a date field
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"DateExtract","Inputs":"TradeDate","component":"year","types":"str"}

  Formula         – free-form numeric expression; use A, B, C as positional placeholders for Inputs
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"Formula","Inputs":"col1,col2","formulastring":"A * B / 100","types":"float"}

  MapValues       – dictionary lookup
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"MapValues","Inputs":"StatusCode","mapping":{"A":"Active","I":"Inactive"},"types":"str"}

  StringOperation – upper | lower | strip | length | title
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"StringOperation","Inputs":"col","operation":"upper","types":"str"}

  NumericOperation– sum | mean | max | min | std across Inputs columns
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"NumericOperation","Inputs":"col1,col2","operation":"sum","types":"float"}

  CopyField       – copy a field verbatim
                    {"NewFieldName":"out","Operation":"CopyField","Inputs":"source_col","types":"str"}

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
AND-CONDITION CHAINING
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Conditional tests ONE field per call. For "field A AND field B" logic use TWO steps:

  Step 1: test field A → true_value="<final>", false_value="_check_"
  Step 2: pass the full data array from step 1 as-is; test the "_check_" column
          → if "_check_" → apply field B logic; else keep the step-1 value.

  Example: Notional >= 10M AND Severity == "High" → "Critical"
    Step 1 operation: Inputs="Notional", comparator=">=", value="10000000",
                      true_value="Critical", false_value="_check_"
    Step 2 operation: Inputs="severity_score" (step-1 output), comparator="==", value="_check_",
                      true_value="Elevated_or_Normal_by_Severity", false_value="Critical"
    (Add a Step 3 if you need the Severity → Elevated vs Normal branch.)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 1 — DESIGN THE OPERATION
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Based on the plan:
1. Select the correct Operation type (or chain of types for AND rules).
2. Write out the complete operation JSON for each step.
3. Present the design to the user in a clear code block BEFORE running any tools.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 2 — GENERATE TEST CASES
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Create exactly 5 test rows. If is_multi_condition is true, ensure every branch combination is covered.

Must cover:
  TC-1  Happy path — all inputs valid, produces expected primary output
  TC-2  Second branch — inputs that trigger a different output branch
  TC-3  Third branch (if exists) — or null/empty input edge case
  TC-4  Boundary value — exactly at a numeric threshold, max-length string, special chars
  TC-5  Unexpected / default — value not in any branch, or null that should yield a default

Show the 5 test cases as a markdown table (id | input fields | expected output) BEFORE calling any API.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 3 — VALIDATE VIA /transform/execute
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Call POST /transform/execute with body nested correctly:
  {"data": [<your 5 test rows>], "operation": <step-1 operation>}

CRITICAL — body structure:
  Correct:  api_call(endpoint="/transform/execute", method="POST",
                     body={"data":[...], "operation":{...}})
  Wrong:    api_call(endpoint="...", data=[...], operation={...})   ← top-level keys fail

After receiving the response:
  - Check success == true.
  - Read EVERY row in response.data and extract the value of NewFieldName.
  - Never say "results not retrieved" if success=true — they are in data[*].<NewFieldName>.

For AND-condition chains:
  - Run Step 1, read all data[*].<step1_NewFieldName> values.
  - Pass the ENTIRE response.data array unchanged as the data input for Step 2.
  - Read data[*].<step2_NewFieldName> for the final scores.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 4 — REPORT RESULTS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
After all API calls complete, show a result table:

  | TC  | Input Summary         | Expected       | Got            | Status |
  |-----|-----------------------|----------------|----------------|--------|
  | TC-1| field=value, ...      | Critical       | Critical       | ✅ PASS |
  | TC-2| ...                   | Elevated       | Elevated       | ✅ PASS |
  | TC-3| ...                   | Normal         | Normal         | ✅ PASS |
  | TC-4| ...                   | Critical       | Critical       | ✅ PASS |
  | TC-5| ...                   | Normal         | Normal         | ✅ PASS |

If any test fails:
  - Explain WHY the formula produced the wrong output.
  - Propose a corrected operation JSON.
  - Offer to re-run the test automatically.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
STEP 5 — FINAL OUTPUT
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
If all 5 tests pass, output:
  1. A copy-paste-ready operation JSON block (or array of steps for AND-chains).
  2. A one-line plain-English description of what the formula does.
  3. If the user mentioned a config file path earlier, ask: "Shall I write this to your config file?"

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
- Present design and test table BEFORE calling any tool.
- Always nest data+operation inside body — never at top level.
- For chained steps pass the full data array from the previous response unchanged.
- Read data[*].NewFieldName to extract results — do not assume results are unavailable."""

       SLIDE 2 — What Is It?
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-2">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Overview</div>
      <h2>What Does It Do?</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:20px;">
        <div class="card accent-red">
          <h3>Single Endpoint, Many Capabilities</h3>
          <p><code>POST /api/ai/chat</code> accepts a conversation, detects what the user needs, orchestrates the right skills and tools, and streams the reply back in real time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-blue">
          <h3>Works Like a Smart Dispatcher</h3>
          <p>It reads the latest user message, picks the right "skill", calls any necessary tools (file read, API call, shell), and sends everything to the LLM to produce a final answer.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="cols cols-3">
        <div class="card">
          <div style="font-size:26px;margin-bottom:8px;">💬</div>
          <h3>Streaming Response</h3>
          <p>Replies are sent character-by-character via Server-Sent Events so the user sees output as it is generated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card">
          <div style="font-size:26px;margin-bottom:8px;">🧩</div>
          <h3>Plug-in Skills</h3>
          <p>Skills are TOML or Markdown files dropped in a folder. Auto-detected from keywords in the user's message.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card">
          <div style="font-size:26px;margin-bottom:8px;">🔒</div>
          <h3>Security at Code Level</h3>
          <p>File, shell, and API access is enforced by Python code — not by AI instructions. The agent cannot bypass it.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

      <div class="arch-diagram">
        <div class="arch-row">
          <div class="arch-box browser" style="max-width:220px;">🌐 Browser / React App<small>ChatArea.js — POST + SSE reader</small></div>

        </div>
        <div class="arch-line">↓</div>
        <div class="arch-row">
          <div class="arch-box skill">🧩 Skill Planner<small>parallel asyncio.gather</small></div>
          <div class="arch-line">→</div>
          <div class="arch-box fastapi">🤖 LLM (OpenRouter)<small>chat completions API</small></div>
          <div class="arch-line">→</div>

        </div>

        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-top:16px;">
        <p style="font-size:13px;">📦 <strong>All in one process.</strong> Tool calls to internal APIs use in-process ASGI transport — zero network hops. The LLM provider (OpenRouter) is the only external call.</p>
        <p style="font-size:13px;">🔒 <strong>Security is in the code, not in the AI.</strong> The agent produces JSON describing what it wants to do. Python code runs security checks before anything executes.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 4 — Complete Request Flow (Visual)
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-4">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Request Lifecycle</div>
      <h2>Complete Request Flow — Step by Step</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2">
        <div class="flow-diagram">
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num red">1</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Receive &amp; Parse Request</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">Browser sends <code>POST /api/ai/chat</code> with conversation history, model, temperature, environment, and optional skill names. The latest user message is extracted from the history.</div>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num blue">2</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Detect Skills</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">Each skill's trigger keyword list is matched against the user message (lowercase). Explicit <code>active_skills</code> from the frontend are merged in. Result: a list of skill names to activate.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">detect_requested_skills()</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num purple">3</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Plan Skills — In Parallel</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">Each skill's planning prompt is sent to the LLM simultaneously using <code>asyncio.gather</code>. Returns a structured JSON plan per skill. All planners run at the same time — no extra latency per skill.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">run_skill_planning_turn()</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num amber">4</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Compact &amp; Trim History</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">If history exceeds 70% of the context window, older messages are summarised by the LLM into one block. Then the last 20 messages are kept verbatim. A <code>was_compacted</code> flag is recorded for the UI.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">_compact_messages() → _trim_messages()</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num green">5</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Tool Loop or Direct Answer</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">If the skill plan or user message indicates tools are needed, the tool loop runs (up to 3 turns, concurrent tool execution per turn). Otherwise a single LLM call produces the final answer.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">run_tool_enabled_chat()</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num red">6</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Stream Response via SSE</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">The answer is split into 50-char chunks, each sent as a <code>data: {"content": "…"}</code> SSE event. The user sees text appearing in real time. Tool-call labels are emitted before tool execution starts.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">emit_sse_chunks()</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num blue">7</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Emit <code>done</code> Event</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">After the last content chunk, a <code>done</code> event carries token usage (prompt / completion / total) and whether compaction occurred. The frontend uses this to display the token stats bar.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">{"done": true, "usage": {…}, "compacted": bool}</span>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="flow-step">
            <div class="flow-num slate">8</div>
            <div class="flow-content">
              <div class="flow-title">Background Quality Judgment (20%)</div>
              <div class="flow-desc">One in five responses is scored 0–100 by a separate LLM call on criteria like accuracy and conciseness. Runs as an async background task — the user never waits for it.</div>
              <span class="flow-badge">asyncio.create_task(_silent_judge_and_save())</span>
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>

        <!-- Right column: branching detail -->
        <div>
          <h3 style="margin-bottom:12px;">Tool Loop Detail (Step 5)</h3>
          <div class="card" style="background:#f8fafc;margin-bottom:14px;">
            <div style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--slate);line-height:1.8;">
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;margin-bottom:6px;">
                <span style="background:var(--blue);color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">A</span>
                <span>LLM receives messages + tool definitions</span>
              </div>
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;margin-bottom:6px;">
                <span style="background:var(--purple);color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">B</span>
                <span>LLM responds with JSON tool calls (text output only)</span>
              </div>
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;margin-bottom:6px;">
                <span style="background:var(--red);color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">C</span>
                <span><strong>Security gate runs</strong> — path / shell / API checks</span>
              </div>
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;margin-bottom:6px;">
                <span style="background:var(--amber);color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">D</span>
                <span>Approved tools run concurrently (<code>asyncio.gather</code>)</span>
              </div>
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;margin-bottom:6px;">
                <span style="background:var(--green);color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">E</span>
                <span>Results appended as <code>role=tool</code> messages</span>
              </div>
              <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;">
                <span style="background:#64748b;color:#fff;border-radius:50%;width:20px;height:20px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;flex-shrink:0;">↺</span>
                <span>Repeat A–E up to 3 turns, then force-finish</span>
              </div>
            </div>
          </div>

          <h3 style="margin-bottom:12px;">SSE Event Sequence</h3>
          <div class="card" style="background:#0f172a;padding:14px 16px;font-family:monospace;font-size:12px;line-height:1.9;color:#e2e8f0;">
            <div style="color:#64748b;">// Tool call label</div>
            <div>data: {"tool_call":"read","tool_label":"Reading file…"}</div>
            <div style="color:#64748b;margin-top:6px;">// Content chunks (50 chars each)</div>
            <div>data: {"content":"The configuration file has "}</div>
            <div>data: {"content":"3 errors in the Rules sheet."}</div>
            <div style="color:#64748b;margin-top:6px;">// Done event with usage</div>
            <div>data: {"done":true,"usage":{…},"compacted":false}</div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 5 — Security: The Code Wall
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-5">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Security Architecture</div>
      <h2>The Code Wall — Security Is Not in the Agent's Hands</h2>

      <div class="card accent-red" style="margin-bottom:18px;">
        <h3>Critical Design Principle</h3>
        <p>The AI model is a <strong>text prediction engine</strong>. It produces JSON describing what tool it wants to call. It cannot execute anything itself. Every tool call passes through <strong>Python security checks before any action is taken</strong>. The agent cannot bypass, override, or modify these checks — they are not instructions to the AI, they are code.</p>
      </div>

      <div class="wall-container">
        <div class="wall-box wall-agent">
          <div style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:1px;text-transform:uppercase;color:#a78bfa;margin-bottom:8px;">🤖 LLM / Agent Layer (text only — no execution rights)</div>
          <div style="font-size:12.5px;color:#ddd6fe;line-height:1.7;">
            The model outputs JSON like: <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#c4b5fd;padding:1px 6px;border-radius:4px;">{"name":"read","filePath":"/etc/passwd"}</code><br/>
            That JSON is just a string. The model has not read anything yet. It cannot read anything directly.
          </div>
        </div>

        <div class="wall-wall">⬇ &nbsp; JSON TEXT CROSSES THE CODE WALL &nbsp; ⬇</div>

        <div class="wall-box wall-code">
          <div style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:1px;text-transform:uppercase;color:#6ee7b7;margin-bottom:10px;">🛡️ Python: execute_tool() — ai_tools.py (operator-controlled)</div>
          <div class="wall-check">
            <div class="wall-check-icon fail">✗</div>
            <div class="wall-check-text"><strong>Path check:</strong> <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">/etc/passwd</code> resolves to a path not in <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">AI_ALLOWED_PATHS</code> → <strong style="color:#fca5a5;">Access Denied</strong></div>
          </div>
          <div class="wall-check">
            <div class="wall-check-icon fail">✗</div>
            <div class="wall-check-text"><strong>Shell check:</strong> command contains <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">| bash</code> or <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">rm -rf</code> → <strong style="color:#fca5a5;">Blocked by pattern regex</strong></div>
          </div>
          <div class="wall-check">
            <div class="wall-check-icon fail">✗</div>
            <div class="wall-check-text"><strong>API check:</strong> endpoint starts with <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">https://</code> → <strong style="color:#fca5a5;">External URLs denied</strong></div>
          </div>
          <div class="wall-check">
            <div class="wall-check-icon fail">✗</div>
            <div class="wall-check-text"><strong>Command allowlist:</strong> <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">curl</code> not in <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">AI_SHELL_ALLOWED_COMMANDS</code> → <strong style="color:#fca5a5;">Command blocked</strong></div>
          </div>
          <div class="wall-check">
            <div class="wall-check-icon pass">✓</div>
            <div class="wall-check-text"><strong>Approved path:</strong> <code style="background:rgba(0,0,0,0.3);color:#6ee7b7;">D:\office\data\config.xlsx</code> is inside allowed path → <strong style="color:#6ee7b7;">Execute tool</strong></div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-top:16px;">
        <div class="card accent-green">
          <h3>What Operators Control</h3>
          <ul style="margin-top:6px;">
            <li>Which folders the AI can read/write (<code>AI_ALLOWED_PATHS</code>)</li>
            <li>Whether shell is enabled at all (<code>AI_SHELL_ENABLED</code>)</li>
            <li>Exactly which commands are permitted (<code>AI_SHELL_ALLOWED_COMMANDS</code>)</li>
            <li>Which API endpoints the AI can call (<code>AI_API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES</code>)</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-red">
          <h3>What the Agent Cannot Do</h3>
          <ul style="margin-top:6px;">
            <li>Modify its own security checks (they are Python code, not AI instructions)</li>
            <li>Access paths outside the allowlist, no matter what it is told</li>
            <li>Execute external HTTP calls from the api_call tool</li>
            <li>Run destructive shell patterns (rm -rf, pipe to bash, etc.)</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 6 — Security Technical Detail
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-6">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Security — Technical Detail</div>
      <h2>How Each Security Check Works</h2>

      <div class="cols cols-2" style="gap:16px;">
        <div>
          <div class="card accent-blue" style="margin-bottom:14px;">
            <h3>📁 File Path Control</h3>
            <p style="font-size:13px;margin-bottom:8px;">Every file tool call runs <code>_check_path()</code> before touching the filesystem:</p>
            <ol style="font-size:12.5px;padding-left:16px;color:var(--slate);">
              <li>Resolve the path with <code>Path.resolve()</code> — eliminates <code>../</code> traversal attempts</li>
              <li>Compare against each path in <code>AI_ALLOWED_PATHS</code></li>
              <li>Reject if the resolved path is not equal to or inside an allowed directory</li>
            </ol>
            <p style="font-size:12px;margin-top:8px;color:var(--muted);">Even if the AI supplies <code>../../etc/passwd</code>, the resolved path will not match any allowed directory and the call is blocked before any file I/O.</p>
          </div>

          <div class="card accent-amber">
            <h3>🐚 Shell Command Control</h3>
            <p style="font-size:13px;margin-bottom:8px;"><code>_check_shell()</code> runs three independent guards:</p>
            <ol style="font-size:12.5px;padding-left:16px;color:var(--slate);">
              <li><strong>Kill switch</strong> — if <code>AI_SHELL_ENABLED=false</code>, all shell calls return an error immediately</li>
              <li><strong>Pattern blocklist</strong> — regex scans for <code>| bash</code>, <code>| sh</code>, <code>rm -rf</code>, <code>`cmd`</code>, <code>$(…)</code>, and Windows <code>format X:</code></li>
              <li><strong>Command allowlist</strong> — if <code>AI_SHELL_ALLOWED_COMMANDS</code> is set, only matching command names pass. Uses <code>shlex.split()</code> to get the real first token — not a simple string split that can be fooled by quoting.</li>
            </ol>
            <p style="font-size:12px;margin-top:8px;color:var(--muted);">Commands run with <code>shell=False</code> in subprocess — there is no OS shell to inject into.</p>
          </div>
        </div>

        <div>
          <div class="card accent-purple" style="margin-bottom:14px;">
            <h3>🌐 API Call Control</h3>
            <p style="font-size:13px;margin-bottom:8px;"><code>_check_api_endpoint()</code> enforces two rules:</p>
            <ol style="font-size:12.5px;padding-left:16px;color:var(--slate);">
              <li><strong>No external URLs</strong> — any endpoint beginning with <code>http://</code> or <code>https://</code> is rejected. The AI can only supply relative paths like <code>/api/validator/run</code>.</li>
              <li><strong>Prefix allowlist</strong> — if <code>AI_API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES</code> is set, the endpoint must start with one of the listed prefixes.</li>
            </ol>
            <p style="font-size:12px;margin-top:8px;color:var(--muted);">All approved API calls go through the in-process ASGI transport — they reach the same FastAPI app directly, never leaving the server.</p>
          </div>

          <div class="card accent-green">
            <h3>✂️ Result Truncation</h3>
            <p style="font-size:13px;margin-bottom:8px;">After every successful tool call, the result string is checked:</p>
            <ul style="font-size:12.5px;padding-left:16px;color:var(--slate);">
              <li>If <code>len(result) &gt; 12,000</code> characters, it is truncated and a note is appended: <em>"[… truncated — N chars total]"</em></li>
              <li>This prevents a large file or command output from flooding the model's context window and causing errors or cost spikes</li>
              <li>Configurable via <code>AI_MAX_TOOL_RESULT_CHARS</code></li>
            </ul>
          </div>

          <div class="card" style="margin-top:14px;background:#f8fafc;">
            <h3>🔑 Key Point for Management</h3>
            <p style="font-size:13px;">These security checks are <strong>immutable from the AI's perspective</strong>. The AI model has no mechanism to change environment variables, edit Python files, or alter its own execution environment. Security configuration is exclusively in operator hands.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 7 — Skills System
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-7">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Skills</div>
      <h2>The Skills System</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:16px;">
        <div>
          <p style="margin-bottom:14px;">Skills are <strong>pre-built AI behaviours</strong> that add specialised capability. Defined as plain files — no code deployment needed to add a new skill.</p>
          <div class="card accent-green" style="margin-bottom:10px;">
            <h3>📄 SKILL.md format</h3>
            <p>Markdown file with YAML frontmatter. The body is the system prompt. Simplest format — just write instructions in plain English.</p>
          </div>
          <div class="card accent-blue">
            <h3>⚙️ TOML format</h3>
            <p>Structured config with <code>triggers[]</code>, a planning prompt template, and a system prompt template. Supports two-step planning for complex skills.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
        <div>
          <h3 style="margin-bottom:10px;">Built-in Skills</h3>
          <div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:8px;">
            <div class="card" style="padding:10px 14px;"><span class="badge badge-green">config_tester</span><p style="font-size:12.5px;margin-top:5px;">Reads Excel config, runs validator API, fixes ERRORs, retests — max 2 turns.</p></div>
            <div class="card" style="padding:10px 14px;"><span class="badge badge-blue">transform_formula</span><p style="font-size:12.5px;margin-top:5px;">Designs field-operation formula, creates 5 test cases, validates, reports pass/fail, restores file.</p></div>
            <div class="card" style="padding:10px 14px;"><span class="badge badge-purple">completeness_steps / flowchart / html_artifact</span><p style="font-size:12.5px;margin-top:5px;">Visual output skills: ETL node instructions, flow diagrams, self-contained HTML apps.</p></div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="card" style="background:#f8fafc;">
        <p style="font-size:13px;"><strong>Parallel planning:</strong> all triggered skills run their planning LLM call concurrently via <code>asyncio.gather</code>. Adding 5 skills does not add 5× latency.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 8 — Tool Engine
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-8">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Tool Execution</div>
      <h2>Tool Execution Engine</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:16px;">
        <div>
          <h3 style="margin-bottom:10px;">Available Tools</h3>
          <table>
            <thead><tr><th>Tool</th><th>Action</th></tr></thead>
            <tbody>
              <tr><td><code>read</code></td><td>Read a text file</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>write</code></td><td>Write / overwrite a text file</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>read_excel</code></td><td>Read a specific Excel sheet</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>write_excel</code></td><td>Write / update an Excel sheet</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>shell</code></td><td>Run a shell command</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>glob</code></td><td>Find files by pattern</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>grep</code></td><td>Search file contents</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>api_call</code></td><td>Call the same FastAPI backend</td></tr>
            </tbody>
          </table>
        </div>
        <div>
          <div class="card accent-amber" style="margin-bottom:12px;">
            <h3>⚡ Concurrent Execution</h3>
            <p>Multiple tools requested in one turn all run simultaneously via <code>asyncio.gather</code>. A 4-tool turn takes as long as the slowest single tool.</p>
          </div>
          <div class="card accent-blue">
            <h3>🔗 In-Process API</h3>
            <p>The <code>api_call</code> tool uses <code>httpx.ASGITransport</code> to call the same FastAPI app in-process — zero network hops, identical in dev and production regardless of deployed URL.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="card" style="background:#f8fafc;">
        <h3 style="margin-bottom:6px;">Max 3 Tool Turns</h3>
        <p style="font-size:13px;">If the LLM still requests tools after 3 turns, a summarise-and-stop call is made. The user always receives a response — the loop never stalls indefinitely.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 9 — Context Management
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-9">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Context Management</div>
      <h2>Context Compaction &amp; History Trimming</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:18px;">
        <div class="card accent-purple">
          <h3>Problem: Finite Context Windows</h3>
          <p>Every LLM has a maximum context size (e.g. 8,192 tokens). Long conversations fill it up. Exceeding the limit causes API errors; sending large contexts wastes cost.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-green">
          <h3>Solution: Two-Stage Management</h3>
          <ol style="padding-left:18px;margin-top:8px;">
            <li style="margin-bottom:6px;"><strong>Compaction</strong> — summarise old messages when &gt; 70% full</li>
            <li><strong>Trimming</strong> — always keep at most 20 messages</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="arch-diagram" style="margin-bottom:16px;">
        <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:0;flex-wrap:wrap;justify-content:center;font-size:13px;">
          <div style="background:#f1f5f9;border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:9px;padding:10px 14px;text-align:center;min-width:110px;font-weight:600;">Full history<br/><small style="font-weight:400;color:var(--slate);">N messages</small></div>
          <span style="padding:0 6px;color:var(--muted);font-size:16px;">→</span>
          <div style="background:#f5f3ff;border:1px solid #ddd6fe;border-radius:9px;padding:10px 14px;text-align:center;min-width:110px;font-weight:600;color:#4c1d95;">Token estimate<br/><small style="font-weight:400;">&gt; 70% window?</small></div>
          <span style="padding:0 6px;color:var(--muted);font-size:14px;">YES →</span>
          <div style="background:#fffbeb;border:1px solid #fde68a;border-radius:9px;padding:10px 14px;text-align:center;min-width:110px;font-weight:600;color:#78350f;">LLM summarises<br/><small style="font-weight:400;">older messages</small></div>
          <span style="padding:0 6px;color:var(--muted);font-size:16px;">→</span>
          <div style="background:#f0fdf4;border:1px solid #bbf7d0;border-radius:9px;padding:10px 14px;text-align:center;min-width:110px;font-weight:600;color:#065f46;">1 summary block<br/><small style="font-weight:400;">+ recent verbatim</small></div>
          <span style="padding:0 6px;color:var(--muted);font-size:16px;">→</span>
          <div style="background:#eff6ff;border:1px solid #bfdbfe;border-radius:9px;padding:10px 14px;text-align:center;min-width:110px;font-weight:600;color:#1e40af;">Trim to last 20<br/><small style="font-weight:400;">messages</small></div>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="cols cols-3">
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">70%</div><div class="stat-label">Compaction threshold</div></div>
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">12%</div><div class="stat-label">Summary target size</div></div>
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">20</div><div class="stat-label">Max messages kept verbatim</div></div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 10 — Token Usage & Streaming
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-10">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Observability</div>
      <h2>Token Usage &amp; Streaming Protocol</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:18px;">
        <div>
          <h3 style="margin-bottom:10px;">SSE Event Types</h3>
          <table>
            <thead><tr><th>Event</th><th>Payload</th><th>Purpose</th></tr></thead>
            <tbody>
              <tr><td><code>tool_call</code></td><td><code>tool_label</code></td><td>Spinning-wrench status in UI</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>content</code></td><td>50-char chunk</td><td>Streams text in real time</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>error</code></td><td>message string</td><td>Surfaces failures cleanly</td></tr>
              <tr><td><code>done</code></td><td><code>usage</code>, <code>compacted</code></td><td>Signals end; carries token counts</td></tr>
            </tbody>
          </table>
        </div>
        <div>
          <div class="card accent-blue" style="margin-bottom:12px;">
            <h3>Token Counting</h3>
            <p>A running <code>usage_acc</code> dict accumulates tokens across every LLM call in the request — tool turns, direct answer, and the max-turns fallback. The total is sent in the <code>done</code> event.</p>
            <p style="margin-top:8px;font-size:12.5px;color:var(--muted);">● 1,234 prompt &nbsp;● 512 completion &nbsp;<strong style="color:var(--navy);">1,746 total</strong></p>
          </div>
          <div class="card accent-green">
            <h3>Singleton Client</h3>
            <p>One <code>AsyncOpenAI</code> instance is shared across all requests, preserving the connection pool and avoiding TLS handshake overhead per request.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 11 — Quality Judgment
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-11">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Quality</div>
      <h2>Quality Judgment Service</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:18px;">
        <div class="card accent-red">
          <h3>What Is It?</h3>
          <p>After every chat response, there is a <strong>20% chance</strong> a second LLM call scores the quality of the answer 0–100. Runs in the background — the user never waits.</p>
          <p style="margin-top:10px;">A smaller/cheaper model is used for judgment. The score and reasoning are saved for trend analysis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-blue">
          <h3>Evaluation Criteria</h3>
          <ul style="margin-top:6px;">
            <li><strong>Technical Accuracy</strong> — factually correct, no deprecated practices</li>
            <li><strong>Conciseness</strong> — brief, every sentence adds value</li>
            <li><strong>Completeness</strong> — all parts of the question answered</li>
            <li><strong>Code Quality</strong> — best practices, error handling, readable names</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="cols cols-3">
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">20%</div><div class="stat-label">Sample rate (configurable)</div></div>
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">0–100</div><div class="stat-label">Score range</div></div>
        <div class="card"><div class="stat-num">≥60</div><div class="stat-label">Pass threshold</div></div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 12 — Configuration
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-12">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Configuration</div>
      <h2>Environment Configuration</h2>
      <p style="margin-bottom:14px;">All tuneable parameters are environment variables — no code changes between environments.</p>
      <table>
        <thead><tr><th>Variable</th><th>Default</th><th>Purpose</th></tr></thead>
        <tbody>
          <tr><td><code>OPENAI_API_KEY</code></td><td>—</td><td>API key for OpenRouter (required)</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>OPENAI_API_BASE_URL</code></td><td>openrouter.ai/api/v1</td><td>LLM provider base URL</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_MAX_HISTORY_MESSAGES</code></td><td>20</td><td>Max messages kept after trimming</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_CONTEXT_MAX_TOKENS</code></td><td>8192</td><td>Assumed model context window</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_COMPACTION_THRESHOLD</code></td><td>0.70</td><td>Fraction that triggers compaction</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_COMPACTION_TARGET</code></td><td>0.12</td><td>Summary target fraction</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_ALLOWED_PATHS</code></td><td>—</td><td>Semicolon-separated allowed folders</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_SHELL_ENABLED</code></td><td>true</td><td>Enable/disable shell tool entirely</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_SHELL_ALLOWED_COMMANDS</code></td><td>—</td><td>Comma-separated allowed commands</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>AI_API_ALLOWED_PREFIXES</code></td><td>—</td><td>Semicolon-separated API path prefixes</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>JUDGMENT_ENABLED</code></td><td>true</td><td>Enable/disable quality scoring</td></tr>
          <tr><td><code>JUDGMENT_SAMPLE_RATE</code></td><td>0.2</td><td>Fraction of responses judged</td></tr>
        </tbody>
      </table>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- ══════════════════════════════════
       SLIDE 13 — Summary
  ══════════════════════════════════ -->
  <div class="slide" id="slide-13">
    <div class="slide-inner">
      <div class="slide-tag">Summary</div>
      <h2>Key Takeaways</h2>
      <div class="cols cols-2" style="margin-bottom:20px;">
        <div class="card accent-red">
          <h3>🚀 Real-Time Streaming</h3>
          <p>Responses stream character-by-character via SSE. Tool status shown with a spinning-wrench indicator before each tool runs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-blue">
          <h3>🧩 Extensible Without Code Changes</h3>
          <p>Add a SKILL.md or TOML file to a folder, restart the server — the skill appears automatically, triggered by keywords.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-red" style="border-left-color:var(--red);background:#fff1f2;">
          <h3>🔒 Security Is in Our Code, Not the AI</h3>
          <p>The AI cannot bypass path, shell, or API restrictions. They are enforced in Python before any tool executes. Operators control the allowlists via environment variables.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-amber">
          <h3>⚡ Parallel by Design</h3>
          <p>Skill planning, tool execution, and judgment all use <code>asyncio.gather</code>. Multiple tools in one AI turn run simultaneously, not sequentially.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-purple">
          <h3>🧠 Long-Conversation Aware</h3>
          <p>Context compaction keeps long conversations within model limits by summarising old messages. A "compacted" badge appears in the UI when this happens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="card accent-green">
          <h3>📊 Observable by Default</h3>
          <p>Token usage in every response. Compaction flag in every done event. 20% of responses quality-scored and persisted for trend analysis.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="card" style="background:linear-gradient(135deg,#0f172a,#1e293b);border:none;text-align:center;padding:20px;">
        <p style="color:#94a3b8;font-size:13px;">Source: <code style="color:#fca5a5;background:rgba(255,255,255,0.07);padding:2px 8px;border-radius:4px;">api/routers/ai_assistant.py</code> &nbsp;+&nbsp; <code style="color:#fca5a5;background:rgba(255,255,255,0.07);padding:2px 8px;border-radius:4px;">api/routers/ai_tools.py</code></p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <!-- nav -->
  <div class="nav">
    <div class="nav-dots" id="dots"></div>
    <div class="slide-counter" id="counter">1 / 13</div>
    <div class="nav-btns">
      <button class="nav-btn" id="prev" onclick="move(-1)" disabled>← Prev</button>
      <button class="nav-btn" id="next" onclick="move(1)">Next →</button>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<script>
  const TOTAL = 13;
  let cur = 1;
  const dotsEl = document.getElementById('dots');
  const counterEl = document.getElementById('counter');
  const prevBtn = document.getElementById('prev');
  const nextBtn = document.getElementById('next');
  const progFill = document.getElementById('progress');

  for (let i = 1; i <= TOTAL; i++) {
    const d = document.createElement('div');
    d.className = 'dot' + (i === 1 ? ' active' : '');
    d.addEventListener('click', () => goTo(i));
    dotsEl.appendChild(d);
  }

  function goTo(n) {
    document.getElementById(`slide-${cur}`).classList.remove('active');
    dotsEl.children[cur - 1].classList.remove('active');
    cur = Math.max(1, Math.min(TOTAL, n));
    document.getElementById(`slide-${cur}`).classList.add('active');
    dotsEl.children[cur - 1].classList.add('active');
    counterEl.textContent = `${cur} / ${TOTAL}`;
    prevBtn.disabled = cur === 1;
    nextBtn.disabled = cur === TOTAL;
    progFill.style.width = `${(cur / TOTAL) * 100}%`;
  }

  function move(dir) { goTo(cur + dir); }

  document.addEventListener('keydown', e => {
    if (e.key === 'ArrowRight' || e.key === 'ArrowDown') move(1);
    if (e.key === 'ArrowLeft'  || e.key === 'ArrowUp')   move(-1);
    if (e.key === 'Home') goTo(1);
    if (e.key === 'End')  goTo(TOTAL);
  });

  progFill.style.width = `${(1 / TOTAL) * 100}%`;
</script>
</body>
</html>
